(2) `docs/08-planning/feature-proposals.md` — updated FP-054 Status: "54.0 HARDENED at ACT-256 via MIG-105 — REVOKE FROM PUBLIC, anon — conforms to repo SECURITY DEFINER hardening pattern". (b) **AC3 spread paired** — arm-vs-`gated_k0` paired join at T+5 with both sides `price_source_status='success'` → **0 rows** (no successful T+5 maturations yet pre-heal — fetch_error path dominates). (c) **AC4 fetch-error clusters** — `combiner_forward_returns` rows with `price_source_status<>'success'` grouped by `(status, seed_as_of_date)` → **480 rows** with `price_source_status='fetch_error'` on `seed_as_of_date=2026-06-19` (12 variants × 40 names; renders). (d) **AC5 book head** — `DISTINCT ON (variant)` latest `as_of_date` from `combiner_book_shadow` → **12 variants** all at `as_of_date=2026-06-19` with 40 rows per variant (3.M-iii shadow rows carrying). All four executed against the live data contract; hooks de-risked; the four readout shapes are byte-confirmed before 54.1 authoring. |
| **STEP E reference-index + FP Status** | function-index entry added (kill_switch_resume precedent + field table). MIG-104 ledger row added; MIG-103 ledger catch-up row added (no DB change — closes audit-trail gap for `cron_last_fire` migration applied 2026-06-20 without ledger entry). FP-054 Status updated to "54.0 LANDED" with §22.5.1 + STEP D evidence summaries. |
| **Out-of-scope guarantees** | NO frontend (panel is 54.1/54.2). NO DB object beyond the one RPC. NO write to `system_config` or any other table (STEP C/D read-only). NO route to operator out-of-band apply (new migration, standard env → executor migration-tool canonical, §22.3(f)). NO touch to existing SECURITY DEFINER functions. NO new permission / event / env-var / config / dependency. |
| **Capability-gap surface (§22.8.5)** | The Supabase migration-tool `GRANT EXECUTE ... TO authenticated` does not auto-issue `REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM PUBLIC`, so PUBLIC retains the default EXECUTE privilege (visible in Supabase linter WARN 0028 within the broader 28-finding set; not a new finding attributable to this migration). The in-function `has_permission(auth.uid(), 'longshort.view')` gate is the LOAD-BEARING privilege primitive and correctly rejects anon (NULL `auth.uid()` → `has_permission` returns false → `42501 insufficient_privilege`). The supervisor's prompt specified the GRANT pattern verbatim; surfaced here per §22.8.5 rather than altered unilaterally. If the supervisor prefers an explicit `REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC` in a follow-up corrective, the gate would remain the load-bearing element either way. |
| **Gates** | **STEP A anchors** — 5/5 verified, MIG numbering unambiguous (MIG-104 + MIG-103 catch-up). **STEP B migration** — atomic create+apply via `supabase--migration` succeeded. **STEP C §22.5.1 live-DB** — `pg_proc` + `prosrc` gate + NULL-return + GRANT all verified. **STEP D readout validation** — 4/4 queries executed read-only, results consistent with pre-heal state. **STEP E same-PR doc sync** — function-index + ledger (MIG-104 + MIG-103 catch-up) + FP-054 Status + this ACT, all in the same execution turn. |
| **ROI Impact** | Neutral at this commit (read helper, no production behavior change). Forward-enabling: unlocks 54.1/54.2 panel build that surfaces DEC-059 ~90%-of-book gate statistics — AC7 multiplicity guardrails (locked at FP-054) remain the load-bearing decision-integrity element. |
| **Risks / Follow-up** | (1) PUBLIC retains EXECUTE on the new RPC (load-bearing in-function gate mitigates; see Capability-gap surface). (2) MIG-103 ledger catch-up adds no DB change but closes a §22.3(e) same-PR documentation gap retroactively — forward authoring should ledger-record at migration time, not at consumer-time. (3) 54.1 hooks should byte-quote the four STEP D queries; the AC4/AC5 row counts are point-in-time evidence (will grow as 3.M-iii shadow crons accrue), not contract values. |

### ACT-252: FP-053 / DW-106-c-i closure — DEC-060 §(i) clarification (float64-semantics + `carried_forward` provenance) + full-cohort §22.5.1 (b) re-verification

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | ACT-252 (next-free after ACT-251). |
| **Mode** | execution — governance clarification + read-only DB re-verification. **No code change. No schema change. No measurement-parameter change.** |
| **Authority** | Operator-authorized supervisor session 2026-06-20 (DW-106-c-i §22.5.1 (b) resolution, operator-delegated). Pre-data: `heal_date` not stamped; DEC-059 n≥30 window not opened. |
| **HEAD before / after** | before: `df04f293` / after: (Lovable commit at execution close). |
| **Trigger** | §22.5.1 (b) initial 5-sample verification surfaced sub-ULP float drift (≤ 4e-15 absolute) between `carried_forward=true` rows at `as_of=2026-06-18` and their source-publication rows. Drift is inherent to `float8` ↔ JS `number` ↔ JSON round-trip, NOT to the carry orchestrator (which performs **zero** arithmetic on `value`). DEC-060 §(i) prose ("byte-identical") was imprecise vs. the `double precision` storage type the schema actually uses, and §(i)'s `as_of_publication` sketch was superseded by MIG-101's generic `carried_forward` boolean. Operator delegated a documented prose clarification (not a parameter tune) + full-cohort re-verification. |
| **Root cause** | (1) §(i) "byte-identical" was authored against an implicit `numeric` mental model; the implemented schema is `double precision` (verified: `information_schema.columns` → `value` = `double precision`). The carry path holds the value with **zero transformation** (verified: `carry-decider.ts` returns the anchor's value as-is; `carry-orchestrator.ts` passes it through; `missingness-capture.ts` upserts as-is) — the observed drift is a storage-type artifact, not a transformation bug. (2) §(i)'s `as_of_publication` metadata sketch was implemented as the generic `signal_observations.carried_forward` boolean (MIG-101), which is information-preserving (source publication is derivable as the most-recent prior `carried_forward=false AND is_present=true` row). |
| **Change** | (a) **DEC-060 addendum** — §(i) Clarification (i.a + i.b), marked "pre-data, operator-delegated ACT-252; NOT a measurement-parameter change — §(vi) preserved." Explicitly preserves the 22d bound, hold-no-decay, heal_date mechanism, forward-only scope, and `carried_forward` audit-only constraint. (b) This ACT row. **NO code, NO migration, NO schema, NO measurement parameter touched.** |
| **§22.5.1 (b) re-verification (full cohort, read-only, `supabase--read_query`)** | Query: for every `carried_forward=true` row at `signal_id='short_interest_change_30d' AND as_of_date='2026-06-18'`, joined to that ticker's most-recent prior `carried_forward=false AND is_present=true` row. **Compared = 836** (matches orchestrator-reported carried count). **max abs(carried.value − source.value) = 4.88498130835069e-15**. **max abs((carried.value − source.value) / nullif(source.value,0)) = 4.54518278574293e-15**. **PASS** under the clarified float64-semantics contract (≤ 1e-12 relative — observed five orders of magnitude under bound; behaviorally inert for a ranking z-score). |
| **§22.5.1 status (full)** | (a) counts + idempotency PASS (universe 839 / persisted 839 / carried 836 / past_bound 0 / no_publication 3, total=distinct=839). (b) value-verbatim PASS under clarified float64-semantics contract (full-cohort maxima above). (c) absence rows PASS (3 rows; `value=null`, `carried_forward=false`). (d) `heal_date` NOT stamped PASS (manual fn correctly cannot stamp; cron-only per §(iii) / c-ii). (e) reader-isolation PASS (assembler shape returns carried rows as ordinary present values; `carried_forward` not in projection). |
| **FP-053 §22.3(e) status** | **DW-106-c-i CLOSED.** Unblocks DW-106-c-ii (cron + `heal_date` upsert). |
| **Gates** | **Gate 4** — `npx eslint .` → 0 errors (confirms no accidental code touch). No deno gate required (no `.ts` changed). |
| **Out-of-scope guarantees** | Zero edit to `carry-decider.ts`, `carry-orchestrator.ts`, `missingness-capture.ts`, `signal-types.ts`, `longshort-short-interest-carry-compute-manual/index.ts`, any test file, any SQL migration, any schema. Zero change to: 22d bound, hold-vs-decay, heal_date mechanism, forward-only scope, `carried_forward` semantics, any §(i)–§(vi) locked parameter. Zero new permissions / events / configs / env-vars / migrations / routes / dependencies. |
| **ROI Impact** | Neutral — documents the float64-storage reality and the `carried_forward` provenance design. Does NOT weaken the signal contract; under the clarified semantics the carry is contract-compliant by five orders of magnitude. |
| **Risks / Follow-up** | None. An explicit `as_of_publication` column remains deferred (not required for c-ii or the DEC-059 clock); future FP can add it if O(1) forensic provenance is ever needed. |
| **Mode** | execution (Tier-A — test corrective; payload behavior in `missingness-capture.ts` is intentional per ACT-250 and untouched). |
| **Authority** | Operator-authorized supervisor session 2026-06-19 (DW-106-c-i test-corrective build prompt). |

| **Atomic-ish design** | Each cron handler is a single-shot dispatch: `verifyCronSecret` → wall-clock → (POLYGON check on forward-returns only) → `.started` audit → orchestrator `run(as_of)` → terminal audit + response. No partial state on the handler side; orchestrator-internal atomicity (compute-before-write; per-chunk UPSERT) preserved verbatim from 3.M-iii/iv. |
| **Audit envelope (mirrors momentum-compute exactly)** | THREE `writeStrategyAuditEvent` calls per run: `longshort.combiner.shadow_rank.started` / `.completed` / `.failed` (catch path uses `.failed` with `stage:'orchestrator_throw'`) and `longshort.combiner.forward_returns.started` / `.completed` / `.failed`. All carry `trigger:'cron'`. NOT the manual sibling's `manual_triggered`/`manual_completed`/`manual_failed` shape. |
| **Failure-handling contract** | 200-on-`completed` AND 200-on-`failed` (clean orchestrator failure with typed `failure_reason`; the cron run itself succeeded in dispatching). 500 ONLY on orchestrator throw — error codes `cron_combiner_shadow_rank_failed` + `cron_combiner_forward_returns_failed`. Per-ticker `fetch_error` / `polygon_404` rows are NORMAL typed-absence reported in `by_status` metadata (the ACT-245 corrective ensures they retry every run until bars settle); they MUST NOT 500. |
| **Wall-clock discipline** | `as_of = productionClock.getWallClockTs()` is the SOLE wall-clock site in either handler (DEC-034 clause 4). All downstream timestamps derive from `as_of.toISOString()`. Source-sentinel test (2) on each handler greps the code-only (comment-stripped) source for `new Date()` / `Date.now()` / `performance.now()` leaks. |

### ACT-245: FP-052 (Phase 3.M-iv corrective) — Forward-return anti-join filtered to `success` so non-success typed-absence rows retry until bars settle (closes latent cron-timing return-loss; pairs with 3.M-v cron arming)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | ACT-245 (next-free after ACT-244). |
| **Mode** | execution (Tier-A one-line correctness corrective + regression test + governance). Strictly additive on the orchestrator side: only `.eq('price_source_status', 'success')` added to the Step-3 anti-join read; accruer, maturation floor, constants, schema, manual edge fn ALL UNCHANGED. |
| **Tier** | A — under the 3.M-v daily cron (forward-returns at 03:00 UTC Tue–Sat, shadow-rank at 23:30 UTC Mon–Fri), the legacy anti-join would treat the inevitable first-cron `fetch_error` row (written at `run_date == seed + H` cal days, BEFORE Polygon settles the H-th trading day's close) as terminal and permanently lock the now-computable T+H return out of recomputation — systemic ~100% typed-absence across all horizons. Closes the correctness hole BEFORE arming the cron. |
| **Branch** | main (Lovable bridge HEAD). |
| **HEAD before** | `6ea29ad7` (3.M-iv built + deployed at ACT-244; §22.5.1 live-DB verification CLEAN; 520 success rows from manual `as_of=2026-06-19` smoke). |
| **HEAD after** | (Lovable commit at execution close). |
| **Authority** | Operator-authorized supervisor session 2026-06-19 (3.M-iv maturation corrective build prompt; pairs with (must precede) 3.M-v cron arming). |
| **Scope (6 files, strict)** | EDIT: (1) `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-combiner/forward-return-orchestrator.ts` (+1 line `.eq('price_source_status', 'success')` on the Step-3 `fetchAllRows` read + brief contract comment); (2) `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-combiner/forward-return-orchestrator_test.ts` (mock pagedBuilder honors `.eq('price_source_status', …)`; forch-1 existing-FR row stamped with `price_source_status: 'success'`; +1 new test `(forch-6) maturation-retry contract`); (3) `docs/08-planning/deferred-work-register.md` (DW-110 logged — observability + Polygon-budget follow-up); (4) `docs/04-modules/longshort/design-source/phase-3m-shadow-measurement.md` (maturation-retry semantics note added); (5) `docs/08-planning/feature-proposals.md` (FP-052 Status line appended with the ACT-245 corrective + DW-110 reference + combiner deno-test 99 → 100); (6) EDIT this file (ACT-245). |
| **STEP A anchors (verbatim)** | (a) `forward-return-orchestrator.ts` Step-3 read (pre-patch lines 237-244): `existingRows = await fetchAllRows<ExistingFRKey>((from, to) => ctx.supabase.from('combiner_forward_returns').select('source_table, variant, seed_as_of_date, ticker, horizon_td').eq('operator_id', ctx.operator_id).in('seed_as_of_date', seedDates).range(from, to))` — this is the SOLE site the corrective patches; the new `.eq('price_source_status', 'success')` is interposed before `.in('seed_as_of_date', …)`. (b) `forward-return-orchestrator.ts` line 301 UPSERT conflict target: `const onConflict = 'operator_id,source_table,variant,seed_as_of_date,ticker,horizon_td'` — confirms a later `success` row OVERWRITES a prior `fetch_error` / `polygon_404` row by PK (no duplicate row). (c) `forward-return-accruer.ts` `horizon_idx >= bars.length` branch returns `PRICE_STATUS_FETCH_ERROR` with all numerics NULL (and the `bars === null` / `'error'` / `seed_idx < 0` branches likewise emit typed-absence — confirming NO accruer change is needed; the bar array IS the authoritative maturation signal per the constants-file contract, the orchestrator just needed to honor that contract on retry). |
| **Correctness rationale** | Pre-corrective: anti-join skips any tuple with an existing FR row regardless of status → first cron run at `run_date == seed + H (cal)` writes `fetch_error` (bars end at `run_date − 1`; horizon trading day = `run_date` is pre-market on Polygon at 03:00 UTC) → second cron run anti-joins that row out → matured T+H return PERMANENTLY LOST → measurement series is ~100% typed-absence under the daily cadence. Post-corrective: anti-join only skips `success` rows → typed-absence rows are re-attempted every run → `onConflict` UPSERT overwrites the typed-absence row in place the moment the horizon bar settles → matured returns are NEVER lost. Permanent gaps (delisted, halted long-term, ticker change) remain terminal typed-absence indefinitely — the correct DEC-059 outcome (the tuple drops out of both numerator and denominator of every (V − live_gated) pairing). |
| **Out-of-scope guarantees** | Zero edit to accruer, constants, maturation floor, schema, CHECK constraints, `combiner_forward_returns` rows already on disk (the 520 `success` rows from ACT-244's smoke remain anti-joined — NOT re-fetched). Zero `horizon_pending` enum addition (deferred to DW-110). Zero cron arming (3.M-v separate FP-prompt). Zero touch on the manual edge fn, the shadow-rank orchestrator, the live combiner, or `momentum-orchestrator`. Zero new dependency. Zero MIG. |
| **Gate 2 — Deno tests** | `deno test --allow-net --allow-env --no-check _shared/longshort-combiner/forward-return-orchestrator_test.ts` → 6/6 PASS (forch-1..forch-6). Full combiner suite → 100/100 PASS (was 99 at ACT-244 HEAD; +1 from the new `(forch-6) maturation-retry contract` regression case). |
| **Gate 4 — ESLint** | `npx eslint .` → 0 errors, 15 warnings (all pre-existing — sudo-audit + RoleDetailPage + quarterly-refresh-orchestrator unused-disable / exhaustive-deps; identical to ACT-244 baseline; no warnings introduced or removed by this corrective). |
| **DW-110 (observability follow-up)** | `horizon_pending` status enum (CHECK migration) to distinguish not-yet-settled from terminal typed-absence; optional permanent-gap retry bound or `MATURATION_FLOOR_CAL_DAYS[H] = H + 1` as a Polygon-budget tweak. Correctness is closed at this commit; the deferred item is observability + budget only. Trigger: post-DW-109 promotion decision, or when Polygon call-budget review flags the retry volume. |

(c) `combiner_forward_returns` PK = `(operator_id, source_table, variant, seed_as_of_date, ticker, horizon_td)`; CHECKs: `horizon_td ∈ {1,5,20}`, `side ∈ {long,short}`, `source_table ∈ {combiner_book, combiner_book_shadow}`, `price_source_status ∈ {success, polygon_404, fetch_error}`, typed-absence `((status='success' AND raw NOT NULL AND signed NOT NULL AND horizon_close_date NOT NULL) OR (status<>'success' AND raw IS NULL AND signed IS NULL))`. (d) `grep -n "live_gated" docs/decisions/DEC-059-dw109-resolution-rule.md` → lines 28 + 34 confirm the live-book label is `live_gated`. (e) `combiner_book` columns: `(operator_id, as_of_date, side, rank_within_side, ticker, score, ranker_source, computed_at)` — NO `variant` column (job stamps `LIVE_VARIANT_LABEL='live_gated'`); `combiner_book_shadow` has `variant`. (f) `shadow-ranker-orchestrator.ts:308-330` — chunked UPSERT with `UPSERT_CHUNK_SIZE=500` and `computed_at = as_of.toISOString()`; manual handler dual-audit envelope (`manual_triggered` → `manual_completed`/`manual_failed`); both mirrored verbatim. |
| **Atomic-ish design** | All rows computed in memory BEFORE the first UPSERT (mirrors 3.M-iii compute-before-write). Per-ticker fetch failures DO NOT propagate (`'error'` bar bundle → typed `fetch_error` row); one ticker NEVER crashes the run. UPSERT failure within a chunk returns `outcome:'failed'` with previously-written rows counted — partial write is by-chunk, not partial-within-row (every individual UPSERT is atomic on PostgREST's side). |
| **Mandatory pagination** | `combiner_book` + `combiner_book_shadow` + `combiner_forward_returns` (anti-join key) reads all go through `fetchAllRows` — page size 1000, short-read termination. A raw `.select()` on either book would silently truncate to ≤1000 seeds (the 3.0b-ii defect on `feature-assembler-orchestrator.ts`; ACT-237 corrective). |
| **Dedup-by-ticker** | Survivors are deduped to distinct tickers BEFORE the Polygon fan-out; one fetch per ticker even when (live × 12 shadow × {1,5,20}) tuples reference it. With ~480 book rows × 3 horizons before anti-join and ~200 distinct tickers per as_of, the per-run Polygon call count is ~200 — well within the existing budget that the daily signal stack already consumes. |
| **Maturation floor** | Pre-fetch pruning at `run_date − seed_as_of_date ≥ MATURATION_FLOOR_CAL_DAYS[H]` with `{1:1, 5:5, 20:20}`. H trading days always span ≥ H calendar days (a trading day IS a calendar day; weekends/holidays only widen the spread), so the floor never excludes a matured tuple. The bar array is the AUTHORITATIVE maturation check — the floor is an optimization, not a correctness gate. Explicitly NOT the `ceil(H × 1.45)` over-tight pre-filter (would exclude matured tuples in low-holiday windows). |
| **Wall-clock discipline** | `computed_at = as_of_run.toISOString()` per DEC-034 (4) — no `Date.now()` / `new Date()` anywhere in the orchestrator or accruer. Only `productionClock.getWallClockTs()` in the manual handler (sole sanctioned site for future-`as_of` rejection). |
| **`-999` sentinel discipline** | `accrueReturns` writes typed-absence as `NULL` on every non-success row (polygon_404, fetch_error, seed_bar_missing, horizon_bar_missing). Asserted by `acc-8` ("never emits -999 across all failure modes"). The `combiner_forward_returns_typed_absence_chk` CHECK would reject any -999 attempt at the DB layer regardless. |
| **Out-of-scope guarantees** | Zero edit to `PolygonPriceHistoryFetcher`, `momentum-orchestrator`, `shadow-ranker-orchestrator`, `ranker-orchestrator`, `feature-assembler-orchestrator`, or any live combiner code. Zero DB change. Zero `types.ts` regen. Zero cron / `job_registry` row (operator-invoked smoke only at 3.M-iv; cron is 3.M-v). NO write to `combiner_rankings_forward_returns` (deferred — does not exist; book-keyed forward returns are the 3.M-iv authoritative emission). |
| **STEP D — deploy + probe** | `supabase--deploy_edge_functions(['longshort-combiner-forward-returns-manual'])` SUCCESS at HEAD `ea7b4b2c`. No-auth POST probe `{"as_of":"2026-06-16"}` returned **401** — confirms the new edge fn is deployed and protected (would be 404 if the auto-deploy bridge had skipped first-commit enqueue per the twice-seen new-edge-fn pattern). |
| **Gates** | **Gate 2** (deno test on combiner dir) — `99 passed | 0 failed` (baseline 86 + 8 acc tests + 5 forch tests). **Gate 4** (eslint project-wide) — `0 errors / 15 warnings` (all 15 warnings pre-existing; identical to baseline). Sentinel-pattern grep on new files: zero `-999` literals; zero wall-clock reads outside the manual handler's `productionClock` future-reject. |
| **ROI Impact** | Net-positive ROI. This job IS the evidence surface for DEC-059's DW-109 promotion decision (resolution of the ~90%-excluded-by-gate problem). Per-ticker dedup (~12× Polygon call savings vs per-tuple naïve) preserves the daily-signal-stack budget. Typed-absence + the CHECK constraint together make a silent-zero corruption of the promotion comparison structurally impossible. Live + shadow paths byte-identical (zero risk to the 3.0c gated book or the 3.M-iii shadow book). |
| **Capability-gap surfacing** | No new gap. The new-edge-fn deploy-lag pattern (twice-seen pre-3.M-iii) did NOT recur — explicit `supabase--deploy_edge_functions` + 401 probe protocol executed in-cycle per §22.8.4. |
| **Status** | Closed (accruer + orchestrator + manual edge fn deployed + 401 probe green; 99/99 combiner Deno tests pass; eslint 0 errors). Live §22.5.1 smoke pending operator-invoked `{as_of:2026-06-16}` JWT call (mature for T+1, partially mature for T+5/T+20 depending on the run cutoff). 3.M-v (DW-109 promotion read-model + shadow-rank cron sibling + FR cron sibling) pending separate build prompt. |

| **DEC-059 §1a clause (verbatim)** | "Evaluation timing. The primary test is evaluated once, at the first scheduled monthly review after n ≥ 30 paired post-DW-106-heal seed-days have accrued for the variant under test. If the variant does not clear all primary + corroboration criteria at that checkpoint, it is rejected for promotion. Re-evaluation of the same variant requires a superseding DEC that (a) cites the empirical reason, (b) pre-specifies the new checkpoint, and (c) applies an alpha penalty (Bonferroni across all looks taken on that variant) to preserve the 5% family-wise rate." Closes the optional-stopping family-wise-rate inflation gap pre-registration was meant to close. |

| **Evidence** | Snapshot analysis at as_of=2026-06-16 against `signal_observations` (NOT `combiner_feature_vectors`, whose excluded rows have empty `features`): (i) universe = 839 distinct tickers with any 9-catalog observation; 838 with n≥1; 834 with both criticals; 140 included by the §4.3.5 gate (`has_c6 ∧ has_c7 ∧ n_noncrit≥3`) — reconciles byte-equal with the 3.0c-ii smoke included-count. (ii) Coverage distribution n=(0..7): names = (1, 0, 4, 448, 246, 96, 35, 9); composite σ per bucket = (—, —, 0.347, 0.553, 0.441, 0.459, 0.428, 0.497) — n=3 has the HIGHEST σ, NOT the thinnest buckets; "thin coverage = high variance" is NOT empirically supported at this as_of. (iii) Variant A (criticals-required + adj = composite × n/(n+3)) overturns 18/20 long-side and 16/20 short-side names vs the baseline gated book. Variant B (no gate) produces the IDENTICAL top-20/bottom-20 because the highest-|adj| ungated names all have both criticals present anyway. (iv) New-entrant profile (all 34 unique new names): every single new entrant has n ∈ {3, 4} (so n_noncrit ∈ {1, 2}); composite |z| range for new longs = [1.121, 1.947], new shorts = [-1.605, -0.919] — MODERATE-thin extreme-composite names that the §4.3.5 floor was excluding. ZERO new entrants with n ≤ 2. (v) `k`-sensitivity at k ∈ {3, 5, 10}: new-entrant counts long=(18, 17, 17), short=(16, 14, 12) — `k` mostly trims the short side; `k=10` still keeps 12/20 short overturns. (vi) Ungated overrun reference (k=0, raw composite, n≥1): 19/20 long and 18/20 short overturns vs baseline; STILL zero new entrants with n ≤ 2 — the "extreme-thin overrun" failure mode is absent at this snapshot. |
| **Out-of-scope guarantees** | Zero code change. Zero migration. Zero persistence — STEP B computation is read-only via `supabase--read_query` and reported in chat only. Zero touch to `signal_observations`, `combiner_feature_vectors`, `combiner_rankings`, `combiner_book`, `combiner_model_registry`. Zero edge-fn change. Zero RBAC / RLS / job_registry / cron change. `ranker.ts` formula is UNCHANGED — DW-109 promotes the rewrite to a future evidence-gated FP. |
| **Caveats (STEP C)** | This is a SNAPSHOT COMPOSITION ANALYSIS — who enters the candidate books, their coverage / score / σ profile, the variance-by-coverage curve. All measurable now. It does NOT yet answer whether the new entrants subsequently MOVED (forward return) — that needs more than the current ~8 days of live data and is the proper T+1 measurement that gates DW-109 promotion. Composition evidence is necessary (establishes magnitude — ~90% of the gated book disagrees with shrinkage) but not sufficient (ROI sign undetermined). |

### ACT-237: FP-052 (3.0b-ii) corrective — paginated PostgREST reads in combiner orchestrator (defeats 1000-row default cap) + prefer-const fix + DW-104 logged

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | ACT-237 (next-free after ACT-236). |
| **Mode** | execution (Tier-A corrective; direct-to-main; atomic single commit). |
| **Tier** | A — orchestrator-read correctness is financial-path (decides which names get features; the §22.5.1 smoke at as_of=2026-06-16 produced `included_count: 0` — every one of 839 names excluded — purely due to unbounded-read truncation, not data health). |
| **Branch** | main (Lovable bridge HEAD). |
| **Authority** | Operator-authorized 2026-06-17 (supervisor session). Root cause reconciled across dual-investigation passes: PostgREST applies a project-wide 1000-row default cap to any `.select()` that omits both `.range()` and `.limit()`; the orchestrator's `signal_observations` load (prior L153-158) was unbounded and silently truncated to an arbitrary 1000-row slice of the ~7505-row expected payload (839 × 9). The pure assembler then interpreted every missing row as `is_present=false` and excluded the ticker. Data confirmed healthy at the failing as_of (D1: signal #6 = 834 present, #7 = 838 present, all `is_present=true`; D2 ticker spot-check confirmed both criticals exist with values; D3 ruled out ticker-format drift). |
| **Scope (verbatim, no helpful refactoring)** | Exactly 5 files. (1) NEW `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-combiner/paginated-read.ts` — `fetchAllRows<T>(build, pageSize=1000)` short-read-terminating loop + hard MAX_PAGES ceiling + `PostgrestPageResult<T>` / `PostgrestPageBuilder<T>` types + `POSTGREST_DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE = 1000` const; per-page errors thrown (no silent empty-result fallback). DB-free / unit-testable (takes a `build(from, to)` factory). (2) EDIT `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-combiner/feature-assembler-orchestrator.ts` — both unbounded reads replaced with `fetchAllRows(...)`: the `signal_observations` load (the active fix) AND the `universe_membership` rows load (same class, defensive — safe at 839 today, would break past 1000). The floor query (`.limit(1)`) stays as-is. (3) EDIT `feature-assembler-orchestrator_test.ts` — (a) `let order` → `const order` (resolves the prefer-const error that broke Gate 4); (b) mocks gain `.range(from, to)` slicing for both `universe_membership` rows-mode and `signal_observations`; (c) NEW `(orch-8)` regression test — 200 tickers × 9 signals = 1800 signal rows (>1000-cap), asserts orchestrator paginates into pages `[0,999]` + `[1000,1999]`, the short final page (length 800) terminates the loop, and `included_count == universe_size` (not 0 — the bug's signature). (4) EDIT `docs/07-reference/function-index.md` — NEW `paginated-read.ts` entry; existing orchestrator entry gets a "Paginated reads (FP-052 corrective)" row + Tests row bumped 7 → 8 + Added-by appended "; paginated-read corrective (ACT-237)". (5) EDIT `docs/08-planning/deferred-work-register.md` — NEW DW-104 entry citing the ~8 latent `longshort-universe` `universe_membership` unbounded reads (seeded-membership-fetcher ×2, universe-membership-fetcher, universe-service, get-eligibility, universe-membership-persister); Constitution §9 escalating-fix rationale (fix the in-scope manifestation now, log the wider audit for a separately-scoped Phase-1 maintenance pass). (6) EDIT `docs/06-tracking/action-tracker.md` — this row. NO migration, NO `src/`, NO `system-state.md`, NO `master-plan.md`, NO permission-index, NO event-index, NO module doc edits, NO repo-wide `eslint --fix` (would touch pre-existing `src/` warnings). |

### ACT-222: FP-050 Phase 4 — Producer-side per-issuer submissions DEDUPLICATION (Path-Q ratified)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | ACT-222. |
| **Mode** | execution (one focused commit). |
| **Tier** | B (producer script restructure; no edge-function bundle change). |
| **Branch** | main (Lovable bridge HEAD). |
| **Authority** | FP-050 Phase 4 pre-arm hygiene; Catalog #48 resource-dedup sibling-rule amendment (this commit, subsequent firing #2). |
| **Motivating run** | 2026-06-14 GHA `insider-discovery` workflow run 27504513965 (post-ACT-221 repopulation drain; pacing floor at 1100 ms verified in producer logs). CANCELLED at ~1h45m wall-time with `Error: The operation was canceled` (GHA `timeout-minutes` ceiling). Pacing held — status-200 across all observed fetches; ACT-221's 429-elimination contract was honored. Queue state at cancel: **4,697 real rows + 55 heartbeats + 0 null_acceptance + 0 null_ticker** across as_of_dates 2026-03-16 → 2026-04-22 (~33% of the intended 63-day window). Observed call count: 4,451 submissions fetches against an estimated ~700 unique issuers under the legacy per-day shape. ACT-215 acceptance contract and ACT-220 ticker contract both held under load; only the producer's iteration shape failed. |
| **Path ratification** | **Path-Q** (per-issuer dedup at producer-time — eliminate the per-accession-day refetch) chosen over Path-B (raise GHA `timeout-minutes` to 4-6h). Operator rejected Path-B on the substance: raising the timeout papers over a real architectural finding; the producer's iteration shape (day-then-accession-within-day, refetching each issuer's submissions feed N times across the backfill window) IS the defect; per-issuer caching is the fix. Wall-time arithmetic: 4,451 paced fetches @ 1100 ms ≈ 82 min (matches the cancelled drain's observed wall-time); ~700 dedup'd fetches @ 1100 ms ≈ 13 min (≥6× reduction). The three rules together (consumer→producer relocation + producer-side pacing + per-resource dedup) close the SEC-rate-ceiling class structurally. Cross-fire caching (Path-Q' — caching submissions reads BETWEEN backfill runs via Supabase table) remains the long-term move; deferred as FP-051 candidate as before. |
| **Scope (verbatim, no helpful refactoring)** | (1) EDIT `scripts/insider-discovery-egress.ts` — add `RunModeSummary` interface (outcomes + total_accessions_processed + unique_issuers_fetched + dedup_ratio + acceptance_xwalk_misses + global submissions_fetch_status); add `runModeWithSummary(mode, deps)` orchestrator; for `daily` mode delegate to `runDiscoveryDay` unchanged (single-day dedup_ratio is structurally 1.0); for `backfill` mode call new internal `runBackfillDedup` two-pass orchestrator (Pass 1 = parse-all-days, no submissions fetches; Pass 2 = unique-issuer submissions enrichment, ONCE per CIK, paced + retried per ACT-221 disciplines; Pass 3 = cross-walk + insert per day against global acceptance map); reduce `runMode` to a thin wrapper around `runModeWithSummary` for backward compatibility (still returns `DayOutcome[]`); CLI swaps `runMode` for `runModeWithSummary` and emits the new dedup-telemetry fields on the `insider_discovery_run_complete` event. (2) EDIT `scripts/insider-discovery-egress_test.ts` — import `runModeWithSummary`; add `(p5)` sentinel asserting NVDA in 3 distinct day partitions ⇒ `submissions/CIK0001045810.json` fetched EXACTLY ONCE (callsByCik counter = 1; unique_issuers_fetched = 1; total_accessions_processed = 3; dedup_ratio = 3); add `(p6)` sentinel asserting cross-walk hits all 3 NVDA accessions across all 3 day partitions with correct per-day acceptance_datetime + ticker stamping. (3) EDIT `docs/ai-failure-modes.md` — Catalog #48: add **Resource-dedup amendment (ACT-222)** row codifying the fetch-once-per-unique-resource-per-fire rule + forward-binding dedup-telemetry requirement; extend **Subsequent firings** row with the ACT-222 entry; extend **Status** row to bind all three sibling rules (relocation + pacing + dedup). (4) EDIT `docs/06-tracking/action-tracker.md` — this row. NO migration (queue schema unchanged). NO edge-function code. NO RLS/RBAC/audit/sweepers/finalizers/signal-math/jobs touch. |
| **Telemetry** | NEW fields on `insider_discovery_run_complete`: `total_accessions_processed` (sum of in-universe entries across all parsed days — the denominator the producer would have fetched per-accession under the legacy per-day shape), `unique_issuers_fetched` (count of distinct padded issuer CIKs fetched ONCE in Pass 2), `dedup_ratio` (numerator / denominator, 0 if no issuers — operator-visible burst reduction), `acceptance_xwalk_misses` (sum of in-universe accessions whose issuer's submissions feed did NOT return an `acceptanceDateTime` for that accession — Catalog #44 §(b) gate firing at xwalk time). Forward-binding (codified in Catalog #48 amendment): any future signal whose producer iterates a rate-limited reference resource MUST surface its dedup ratio in its run-complete event. |

Auto-deploy will not fire on this commit (no `supabase/functions/` path touched). Six-MATCH attestation explicitly NOT required; deploy-gate doctrine honored by this explicit statement (ACT-221 precedent). |

### ACT-209 Rule-8 amendment (cause-assignment narrative correction; original ACT-209 entry above preserved verbatim per Constitution Rule 8)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Amendment scope** | Narrative-correction only — the §22.8.5 sub-class B forward-binding rule, the workflow rename, the `paths:` widening, the four generic worker deploy steps, the canonical-role naming preference, the cleanup SQL, and the BUILD_SHA-threading pattern ALL stand. What is corrected here is the cause-assignment for run `aadb1329-437f-4e67-8ee8-c53ad3a29c2d` — the diagnostic that motivated ACT-209's narrative was a Catalog #45 phantom-defect (post-fire polling truncated 13 minutes before the run's actual terminal stamp). |
| **Authority** | Operator 2026-06-13 ACT-211 ruling: "Append this correction to ACT-209 as a Rule-8 amendment (do NOT delete the original entry — Rule 8 binds)." |
| **Corrected narrative** | The polling evidence trail recorded in ACT-209 (snapshots at T+0, T+~60s, T+~8min showing zero motion) was a TRUE observation over the polling window. The CONCLUSION ("the run is stalled indefinitely → §22.8.5 sub-class B Registration-Dependency Deploy Gap") was wrong for THIS specific run because the polling window terminated ~13 minutes BEFORE the run reached its actual DB-row terminal stamp. The run's authoritative terminal state, observed at the next-turn re-poll: <pre>status: failed<br/>finalized_at: 2026-06-13 21:29:28+00<br/>heartbeat_at: 2026-06-13 21:29:28+00<br/>failure_reason: 'work_list_slice_deadlock_3x: item ''0001058090-26-000045'' threw: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading ''replace'')'</pre>The Q3 work-list 3-strikes guard (`queue-slice-worker.ts:910-922`) fired exactly as designed. The actual cause was an engine-↔-consumer seam mismatch in the insider work-list `processItem` (closed at ACT-211, above). |
| **What stays unchanged in ACT-209** | (a) The §22.8.5 sub-class B catalog entry — workers MUST redeploy on registry-surface changes — remains independently valid as a forward-binding rule; it was simply not the cause of this specific run's failure. (b) The workflow rename to `deploy-edge-functions.yml` and its widened `paths:` filter remain in force — they enforce the sub-class B rule mechanically and would have been correct work regardless of the cause-assignment for `aadb1329`. (c) The four generic-worker deploy steps and the pre-deploy BUILD_SHA threading remain in force. (d) The operator cleanup SQL embedded in ACT-209 still applies to the stranded `aadb1329` run row (or DID, if the operator has already applied it — idempotent on the `status='running'` guard, so a re-application is a no-op now that the row has been flipped to `failed` either by the operator's cleanup SQL or by the run's own terminal-state landing at 21:29:28+00). |
| **Catalog #45 classification** | This is the FIRST FIRING of the new Catalog #45 (post-fire polling truncation misread as stall) — the phantom-defect form of the Catalog #41 family, supervisor-side mirror. The amendment closes the cause-assignment correction loop; the catalog entry binds the forward rule for all future post-fire diagnostics. |
| **Open audit-event question (split off, not part of this amendment's verdict)** | Lovable's polling reported "zero SLICE_FAILED / RUN_terminal events" in `longshort_audit_logs` for run `aadb1329` — yet the DB row's `failure_reason` + `finalized_at` prove the slice-level state machine DID terminate. Investigate whether `longshort_audit_logs` is missing events that fired in-process, OR whether the work-list mode's slice/terminal telemetry routes through a different event family than the audit-event names Lovable queried. ### ACT-212: FP-050 Phase 4 — latent §(b) gap (Form-4 `index.json` missing `acceptanceDateTime`) — `readAcceptance` should consult SGML header as typed fallback (DEFERRED per ACT-211 operator ruling; OPEN — to be opened post-ACT-211 validation re-fire)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | ACT-212 (next-free after ACT-211; allocated as a stub-OPEN entry per ACT-211 operator ruling "(4) ACT-212 opens with C's deferred scope; latent §(b) gap on index.json-missing-acceptance — readAcceptance should consult SGML header as typed fallback; deferred to a focused follow-up, NOT bundled"). |
| **Mode** | OPEN — investigation + execution pending operator ruling AFTER the ACT-211 validation re-fire surfaces real `permanent_skip no_primary_doc` rates against the live universe. |
| **Status** | OPEN — DEFERRED. Not in-flight. Allocates work post-ACT-211 validation. |
| **Authority** | Operator 2026-06-13 ACT-211 ruling — "C deferred to ACT-212 (new entry; do not bundle)". |
| **Scope (proposed; subject to operator ratification when ACT-212 opens)** | (1) `edgar-accession-index-fetcher.ts`'s `readAcceptance(body)` currently consults four `acceptanceDateTime` / `acceptance-datetime` candidates inside `body` and `body.directory`. Live evidence (2026-06-13 EDGAR fetch of accession `0001058090-26-000045` for CMG/Chipotle): the modern Form-4 `index.json` shape for this filer (and likely a population of similar filers) carries NEITHER candidate, and the `acceptanceDateTime` is present ONLY in the `-index-headers.html` SGML wrapper at the same URL prefix. Under the current `readAcceptance` shape the fetcher returns `kind:'ambiguous', acceptance_datetime:null` even when a single `primary_document` resolved cleanly, and `processItem` routes to `permanent_skip no_primary_doc` — a clean terminal outcome but a coverage gap that suppresses real Form-4 rows. (2) Proposed fix: add a typed fallback that fetches `<accession-no-dashes>-index-headers.html` and extracts `<ACCEPTANCE-DATETIME>` from the SGML header when `index.json` lacks acceptance; the fetcher MUST stay typed (no silent string default) and the fallback fetch MUST count against the same SEC fair-access rate bucket. (3) New regression fixture: the Chipotle accession `0001058090-26-000045` real EDGAR shape (`index.json` + `-index-headers.html` content captured verbatim, no synthetic data). (4) Cost: at most one extra HTTP call per `index.json`-missing-acceptance filing; absent measurement of the population frequency, the bound is one extra call per processed item in the worst case (still inside the 5 rps fair-access ceiling). |
| **Blocking dependencies (registered per the deferred-work-register protocol)** | (a) ACT-211 must merge and the validation re-fire must produce live `signal_compute_log` evidence that confirms the §(b)-gap rate is material against the live universe (the (S.4) regression fixture proves the code-path; the live evidence proves the prevalence — only the latter justifies the implementation cost). (b) Operator ratification of the fix scope (single-fallback to SGML headers vs other shapes; rate-bucket accounting). |
| **References** | Authored at ACT-211 operator ruling. Referenced from ACT-211 Incidental Findings (a) and ACT-211 Status. Catalog cross-reference: §22.8.5 sub-class A (BUILD_SHA threading) and sub-class B (Registration-Dependency Deploy Gap) are unrelated — this is a §(b) (dual-date contract) coverage gap, not a deploy-pipeline gap. |
| **Verification Status** | N/A — entry is an OPEN stub. No code change, no test, no five-gate run, no deploy. Allocates work post-ACT-211 validation. |

| **First binding application of §22.8.5 (catalogued as forensic record)** | This is the first live fire of the F2-pre verifier (ACT-201) against a money-path-adjacent edge function. It caught exactly the gap class the four-outcome contract was designed to surface: HEADER_MISSING distinct from MISMATCH. A two-outcome PASS/FAIL verifier would have classified this as "FAIL — redeploy", leading to a redeploy loop that would never converge. The §22.8.5 sub-class A catalog entry codifies the gap class for the forensic record and binds the same-PR "add to deploy workflow" rule for any future money-path edge function. |
| **Docs / references** | `docs/ai-failure-modes.md` — new §22.8.5 sub-class A entry (see Scope). No `function-index.md` / `event-index.md` / `permission-index.md` / `route-index.md` / `database-migration-ledger.md` updates (this commit adds no functions / events / permissions / routes / migrations; the GHA workflow is repo infrastructure, not a registered shared function). |
| **ROI Impact** | Positive enabling only. Lifts the auto-NEEDS-REVISION on F2.c (ACT-205) once operator-action gate is cleared, unblocking F2.d closure and operator validation curl. Signal #4 remains DISARMED through this commit and through F2.d; arms only after operator validation reads clean. Zero touch to prediction / signal / sizing / execution logic; zero touch to runtime code (only deploy infrastructure + docs). |
| **Status** | Complete + verified. GHA secrets landed, workflow fired green at `65f2e0fd`, both `check-deployed-sha` probes returned MATCH (exit 0). ACT-206 auto-NEEDS-REVISION LIFTED; ACT-205 (F2.c) auto-NEEDS-REVISION LIFTED → MERGE RECOMMENDED in its as-landed form; F2.d closure docs may proceed; operator validation curl is next. ### ACT-204: FP-050 Phase 4 F2.b-hardening — real master.idx parser/header-date fix + in-universe semantic-success gate + external-write verification; GHA run #3 100%-heartbeat outcome diagnosed as parser shape drift, not CIK normalization; Signal #4 remains DISARMED

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | ACT-204 (next-free after ACT-203). |
| **Mode** | investigation → hardening execution, per operator instruction. |
| **Authority** | Operator 2026-06-13 GHA run #3 diagnostic/hardening brief; consolidation of F2.b-hardening external-write verification with parse/filter fix. |
| **Diagnosis** | GHA run #3 (`discovery_correlation_id=658b8070-dba7-44f9-881e-cd12b4c81f8b`) produced `days:63`, `rows_inserted:0`, `heartbeats_inserted:63` with EDGAR 200s. Source inspection showed the parser was positional but gated on synthetic header `CIK\|Company Name\|Form Type\|Date Filed\|Filename` and ISO dates; real SEC master.idx uses `CIK\|Company Name\|Form Type\|Date Filed\|File Name` and compact `YYYYMMDD`, causing 0 parsed Form-4 rows before the in-universe predicate. Live row proof: `1045810\|NVIDIA CORP\|4\|20260605\|edgar/data/1045810/0001768670-26-000002.txt`; hardened parser output has `date_filed='2026-06-05'`, `accession_number='0001768670-26-000002'`; master operand `1045810 → 0001045810`; universe operand `NVDA → 0001045810`; predicate operands match. Verdict: parser header/date mismatch; NOT CIK normalization mismatch, NOT form-type literal mismatch, NOT column-index off-by-one. |
| **Changes** | `edgar-daily-index-fetcher.ts` accepts real `File Name` header + compact dates while preserving historical fixture compatibility; `scripts/insider-discovery-egress.ts` loads current universe tickers, resolves ticker→CIK via `EdgarCikMapper`, filters to in-universe padded CIKs, writes padded `issuer_cik`, emits semantic counters (`entries_parsed`, `entries_after_universe_filter`), fails green exit on structural-only success, logs PostgREST insert status/path/attempted rows/`Preference-Applied`, and verifies persisted count by `discovery_correlation_id`. |
| **Tests** | Focused producer suite: `deno test --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read scripts/insider-discovery-egress_test.ts` → `ok \| 17 passed \| 0 failed (15ms)`. Focused parser+consumer suite: `cd supabase/functions && deno test --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read _shared/longshort-signals/insider-transactions/edgar-daily-index-fetcher_test.ts _shared/longshort-signals/insider-transactions/insider-work-list-registration_test.ts` → `ok \| 39 passed \| 0 failed (173ms)`. |
| **Docs / references** | Updated `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/insider-transactions.md`; updated `docs/07-reference/function-index.md`; added `docs/ai-failure-modes.md` Catalog #44 (structural-success metrics without semantic-success metrics). No migration / no MIG ledger entry. |
| **Deploy gate** | F2.b hardening itself is GHA producer + shared parser/test/doc work; no edge-function deploy is required for the GHA script. Shared parser is consumed by edge code, so the F2-pre deployed-SHA MATCH proof re-enters at F2.c before consumer attestation. |
| **ROI Impact** | Positive enabling impact only; Signal #4 remains DISARMED, so no live trading output changes. The change restores the producer's ability to populate actual in-universe discovery rows and prevents a future green-but-zero semantic backfill. |
| **Status** | Complete; operator should re-fire the workflow with explicit `Run workflow` inputs after this commit. Signal #4 STAYS DISARMED. |

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | ACT-201 (next-free after ACT-200; grep-verified at HEAD via `grep -oE "ACT-[0-9]+" docs/06-tracking/action-tracker.md \| sort -u -V \| tail` → ACT-200 latest at allocation). |

F2-pre is the first of the four sub-commits (F2-pre → F2.a → F2.b → F2.c → F2.d); each ships with the four-gate attestation block at HEAD; STOP between. Signal #4 STAYS DISARMED through F2-pre→F2.d. |
| **Scope (F2-pre)** | (1) `supabase/functions/_shared/handler.ts` — `BUILD_SHA: string \| null` resolved once at module init from `Deno.env.get('BUILD_SHA')` with typed-Optional discipline (no sentinel "unknown" string the verifier could mistake for a real SHA); `stampBuildSha(response)` helper applied on EVERY return path (OPTIONS preflight, rate-limit response, 413 body-too-large, handler success, AuthError 401, ValidationError 400, PermissionDenied 403 incl. recent-auth-required 403, 500 unhandled); `__getBuildShaForTesting()` exported for sentinel tests. (2) `scripts/check-deployed-sha.ts` — pure decision function `checkDeployedSha({functionUrl, readSourceSha?, readDeployedSha?})` returns four-outcome discriminated union `CheckOutcome = MATCH \| MISMATCH \| HEADER_MISSING \| SCRIPT_ERROR` (exit 0/1/2/3); CLI default wiring is `readSourceShaFromGit` (via `git rev-parse HEAD`) + `readDeployedShaFromOptions` (via `fetch(url, {method:'OPTIONS'})` — bypasses auth/rate-limit/body-parsing, every handler responds via the shared envelope); `renderOutcome` emits a one-line operator-readable summary; `parseArgs` validates `--function-url=`. (3) `scripts/check-deployed-sha_test.ts` — 7 tests covering all four outcomes (MATCH, MISMATCH, HEADER_MISSING distinct from MISMATCH, SCRIPT_ERROR via null source-sha + via fetch-throw with verbatim-reason assertion), parseArgs (accept + reject), and outcomeToExitCode exhaustiveness. Fully hermetic (test-injection — no network, no git). (4) `docs/ai-failure-modes.md` — new §22.8.5 deploy-replay-lag entry (NEW class) with the FP-050 Phase 4 F1 stale-bundle false start as the originating observation, the codified countermeasure verbatim, and the forward-binding rule pinning every downstream F2 verification step on a MATCH outcome. (5) This entry. |
| **Four-outcome contract (verbatim from `scripts/check-deployed-sha.ts`)** | MATCH → exit 0 (deployed = source). MISMATCH → exit 1 (redeploy and re-verify). HEADER_MISSING → exit 2 (deployment did not declare a BUILD_SHA at all — fix the deploy pipeline; no amount of redeploying fixes it; this is intentionally distinct from MISMATCH so the deploy-pipeline-configuration defect is not buried in a redeploy loop). SCRIPT_ERROR → exit 3 (git unavailable, network failure — reported with verbatim reason for forensics). |
| **Forward-binding rule (this entry codifies)** | **"Every F2 verification step downstream of F2-pre — and every Tier-A / money-path post-deploy attestation thereafter — MUST gate on a MATCH outcome from `check-deployed-sha` against the function being attested."** A four-gate ALL-GREEN block alone is source-attestation, not runtime-attestation. F2.a (queue migration) gates on MATCH against any function the migration's RLS touches; F2.b (discovery script + GHA) is external-egress and gates on MATCH against the consumer side; F2.c (seedWorkItems switch + R1 heartbeat-or-distinction + R2 concurrency-safety regression test) gates on MATCH against `longshort-insider-compute` + `longshort-insider-compute-manual`; F2.d (module doc + final ACT) gates on MATCH against both insider handlers. |
| **R1/R2 carry-forward (codified for F2.c — not implemented at F2-pre)** | R1 (heartbeat-or-distinction) and R2 (concurrency-safety: consume UPDATE predicate `WHERE as_of_date = $1 AND consumed_at IS NULL RETURNING accession_number` driving the work-item INSERT inside one BEGIN/COMMIT; regression test for two concurrent `seedWorkItems` against the same `as_of_date` → one succeeds with N items, one gets zero, never both half) are operator-binding for F2.c per the ratification ruling. F2-pre does not touch the queue table, the discovery script, or seedWorkItems — those land at F2.a/b/c. R1/R2 are recorded here so the F2.c executor cannot drop them. |
| **Files Touched (planned vs actual)** | EDITED: `supabase/functions/_shared/handler.ts` (build-SHA stamping on every response path); `docs/ai-failure-modes.md` (new §22.8.5 entry); `docs/06-tracking/action-tracker.md` (this entry). NO `job_registry` / `cron.job` / `signal_registry` mutation. NO touch to any signal compute / fetcher / orchestrator / handler outside the shared envelope. Signal #4 STAYS DISARMED. |

When `slice_wall < 60s`, the cron cadence — not the slice wall — is the throttle. Reconciled table: paced floor (32k calls / 4.25 rps ≈ 2.1 h) → slice-wall band (~3.1-4.9 h) → **cron-cadence binding floor (320 × 60s ≈ 5.3 h)**. The factor turning paced-floor → slice-wall band is per-item parser CPU + per-row Supabase upsert (~10-30 s on top of ~23.5 s paced). The factor turning slice-wall → binding is **slice-cadence overhead under the `* * * * *` minute-cron** (single shared cron, oldest-across-all-signals picker). Corrected backfill expectation: **~5.3 h queue-drained** (sole insider drain). The 16h overnight window (US close 21:00 UTC → pre-market 13:00 UTC) provides ~10.7 h headroom — one-shot, no multi-night plan. `accessionsPerSlice` and the slice-wall budget are unchanged; only the operator-facing drain figure. | **§(A) — Work-list mode surface (verbatim from `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/queue-worker/queue-config.ts`)** | **Discriminant:** `cfg.mode === 'work-list'` (queue-config.ts:153 union; the `isWorkListMode(cfg)` helper at :211 is the single point-of-truth for runtime branching). **Required config fields when `mode === 'work-list'` (validator at :350-390):** `itemsPerSlice` (positive integer), `callsPerItem` (positive integer), `seedWorkItems` (`WorkListSeedFn`), `processItem` (`WorkListProcessItemFn`), `loadAndCompute` (`WorkListLoadAndComputeFn`). **Forbidden under cross-mode contamination (config-time error):** `fetchAndCompute`, `sliceSize`, `callsPerName` (per-ticker fields); `pagesPerSlice`, `maxPages`, `fetchPage`, `computeFromItems` (sequential-feed fields). **`WorkListItem` (queue-config.ts:216-230):** `{ id: string; payload: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>> }` where `id` is the synthetic-ticker cursor key — MUST be unique within run, deterministic across re-seeds, ORDER-COMPATIBLE with lex sort. For insider: `id = accession_number` (`NNNNNNNNNN-NN-NNNNNN` is lex-sortable). **`WorkListItemResult` (queue-config.ts:232-234):** `{ kind:'processed' } \| { kind:'permanent_skip'; reason: SignalSkipReason; detail: string }`. **`WorkListSeedFn` (queue-config.ts:243-245):** `(args: { asOf: Date }) => Promise<ReadonlyArray<WorkListItem>>`. **Throw discipline (Q5):** seed throw → `failure_reason='seed_failed: <masked verbatim>'`, terminal `failed` run inserted, NEVER half-seeded (queue-work-list-mode_test.ts:200-223 pins this). Empty-array return is VALID — `kind:'started_empty_work_list'`, status `'running'`, zero cursor rows, next slice CAS→finalizing immediately (queue-work-list-mode_test.ts:225-243). **`WorkListProcessItemFn` (queue-config.ts:261-264):** `(args: { item: WorkListItem; asOf: Date }) => Promise<WorkListItemResult>`. **Return-vs-throw encodes Q3:** `{kind:'processed'}` → engine deletes cursor row (Q1 barrier: producer MUST persist consumer-private row BEFORE returning — `insider_form4_rows` upsert here); `{kind:'permanent_skip', reason, detail}` → engine deletes cursor row AND upserts `signal_queue_skips` (Q4 item-scope marker); THROW → transient, cursor preserved with `claimed_at=null` reset at slice end, `item_retries++`. **`WorkListLoadAndComputeFn` (queue-config.ts:274-280):** `(args: { asOf: Date }) => Promise<ReadonlyArray<{ ticker: string; gicsSector: string \| null; result: TickerComputeResult }>>`. Pure (no I/O timestamps, no clock — `asOf` injected); read-only on the consumer-private table; mass-balance 839 (every universe ticker accounted for: value OR typed skip) is CONSUMER's responsibility — the engine never fabricates an absent ticker. **`TickerComputeResult` (queue-config.ts:47-49):** `{ kind:'value'; raw: number } \| { kind:'skip'; reason: SignalSkipReason; detail: string }`. **Heartbeat constant (queue-config.ts:289):** `WORK_LIST_HEARTBEAT_ITEM_INTERVAL = 25` — slice entry + every 25 processed items. **3-strikes / deadlock guard (queue-slice-worker.ts):** `WORK_LIST_SLICE_FAILURE_THRESHOLD = 3` slice-level, reset on ≥1 success; deadlock guard `claimed>0 ∧ succeeded=0` stamps `failure_reason='work_list_slice_deadlock: <masked verbatim>'` on the run row (queue-work-list-mode_test.ts:399-427). **CRITICAL — sibling absence:** the `productionQueueRegistry` (queue-config.ts:465) at HEAD registers PEAD (per-ticker), options-flow (per-ticker), and news-sentiment (sequential-feed). NO sibling work-list consumer exists. **`queue-work-list-mode_test.ts` IS the behavioral spec** — there is no consumer-side reference implementation to copy. The producer in γ commit-1 conforms to the test-pinned engine contract directly. |
| **§(B) — EDGAR layer typed returns end-to-end** | **Daily-index fetcher (`edgar-daily-index-fetcher.ts`):** `fetchDay(date: Date) → DailyIndexResult` = `{ kind:'rows'; entries: DailyIndexEntry[]; date: string }` (HTTP 200, empty/holiday returns `entries:[]` cleanly — NEVER throws on a holiday); `\| { kind:'unavailable'; reason:'data_unavailable'; date: string }` (HTTP 404 — holiday or archive boundary; typed). All other failures throw `EdgarFetchError`. `DailyIndexEntry` (lines 33-49): `{ form_type:'4'\|'4/A'; filer_cik: string (raw, not padded); company_name; date_filed: ISO YYYY-MM-DD; filename; accession_number: NNNNNNNNNN-NN-NNNNNN }`. v1 trading-day approximation = weekends-only (bounded shortfall ≤ 1 day around double-holiday weeks; NYSE-calendar upgrade tracked separately — same §(f) v1-approximation precedent as DEC-057). Form 3/5/10-K/8-K etc. are dropped at the parser (`isForm4` guard at edgar-daily-index-fetcher.ts:105). **CIK mapper (`edgar-cik-mapper.ts`):** `lookupTicker(ticker)` → `{ kind:'resolved'; ticker; cik10; source:'override'\|'snapshot' } \| { kind:'unresolved'; ticker }` (lines 66-68). `INSIDER_CIK_OVERRIDES` ALWAYS win against the snapshot (NXT → Nextracker 1953967, not Nextpower 1852131). Fetch-per-fire (DEC-058 §(f1)) — no staleness. Missing `EDGAR_CONTACT_EMAIL` env → `EdgarConfigurationError` at construction (fail-loud). Reverse lookup needed for the producer: filer_cik (raw, from DailyIndexEntry) → in-universe ticker. Approach: build `Map<padded-CIK, ticker>` from the universe by passing each universe ticker through `lookupTicker` → `padCik(cik10)` → reverse-index; `unresolved` tickers are counted but do not block (the universe ticker simply has no inverse-mapping and any matching filer_cik will not be filtered in). **Accession-index fetcher (`edgar-accession-index-fetcher.ts`):** `fetchIndex({ cik, accession_number })` → `EdgarAccessionIndexResult` (lines 56-79): `{ kind:'resolved'; primary_document; acceptance_datetime: ISO 8601 UTC; filenames[] }` \| `{ kind:'unavailable'; reason:'data_unavailable' }` (HTTP 404) \| `{ kind:'rate_limited' }` (HTTP 429 — typed so the orchestrator can backoff) \| `{ kind:'ambiguous'; filenames[]; eligible_count: 0 \| >1; acceptance_datetime: string \| null }`. The `ambiguous` branch ALSO fires when `acceptance_datetime` is absent (lines 235-246) — DEC-058 §(b) dual-date contract is non-defaultable. 403 / other non-OK → throws `EdgarFetchError`. **`selectPrimaryDocument(filenames)` (lines 160-178):** eligible iff `/\.xml$/i` AND matches NONE of `^[\w-]*index\.xml$/i`, `\.xsd$/i`, `-cal\.xml$/i`, `-def\.xml$/i`, `-lab\.xml$/i`, `-pre\.xml$/i`. `primary_doc.xml` IS included (modern Form-4 primary). Eligible count != 1 → `primary:null` and the result is `kind:'ambiguous'`. NO heuristic tiebreak (INC-70 rule). **Form-4 fetcher (`edgar-form4-fetcher.ts`):** `fetch({ cik, accession_number, acceptance_datetime, primary_document })` → `EdgarForm4FetchResult` (lines 46-50): `{ kind:'rows'; rows: EdgarForm4Row[] }` \| `{ kind:'unavailable'; reason:'data_unavailable' }` (404) \| `{ kind:'rate_limited' }` (429) \| `{ kind:'unparseable'; reason: string }` (surfaced verbatim from parser). 403 → throws `EdgarFetchError`. **Form-4 parser (`edgar-form4-parser.ts`):** `parseEdgarForm4({ xml, accession_number, acceptance_datetime })` → `{ kind:'parsed'; rows: EdgarForm4Row[] } \| { kind:'unparseable'; reason: string }`. Unparseable branches: empty XML body (line 150); absent acceptance_datetime (line 153, §(b) dual-date contract); missing `<issuer>` block (line 162); missing or non-numeric `<issuerCik>` (line 166). Per-row drops (counted-not-errored, do NOT mark the filing unparseable): `transactionDate` absent or non-ISO (line 188); `shares` non-finite or ≤ 0 (line 212). **`EdgarForm4Row` (edgar-form4-parser.ts:47-81):** `{ issuer_cik (padded 10-digit), owner_cik (padded 10-digit; MAY be empty string if `<rptOwnerCik>` absent — line 173 `padCikString(null) → ''`), accession_number, transaction_seq (0-indexed within nonDerivativeTable), transaction_code, shares, price_per_share (may be 0 for gifts), acquired_disposed:'A'\|'D'\|string, ownership_type:'D'\|'I'\|string, officer_title (empty string if absent), is_director, is_officer, is_ten_percent_owner, has_10b5_1_mention (form-level boolean — conservatively applied to every row per §(c)), transaction_date (ISO YYYY-MM-DD), acceptance_datetime (ISO 8601 UTC) }`. **CRITICAL — `owner_cik` semantics on the parser:** the parser's `padCikString(firstTag(ownerIdBlock, 'rptOwnerCik'))` returns `''` when the tag is missing rather than failing the parse (line 173). This is a parser-side LENIENT default that DOES NOT conform to the operator's 3.6b.iii′ ruling ("owner_cik absent → parse failure → permanent_skip"). See §(E) finding 1. |
| **§(C) — `signal_queue_skips` writer surface + Q4 two-ledger semantics (wired at 3.6a.ii, verified at queue-slice-worker.ts:843-870)** | **Engine-side writer (queue-slice-worker.ts:845-857):** `supabase.from('signal_queue_skips').upsert([{ run_id, signal_id: config.signalId, ticker: itemCtx.ticker, skip_reason: result.reason, detail: result.detail, recorded_at: as_of.toISOString() }], { onConflict: 'run_id,ticker', ignoreDuplicates: true })`. The producer-side consumer NEVER writes to `signal_queue_skips` directly — it returns `{kind:'permanent_skip', reason, detail}` from `processItem` and the engine writes the row. **Column shape (from upsert payload):** `run_id` (uuid), `signal_id` (text, matches `productionQueueRegistry` key), `ticker` (text — SYNTHETIC ticker = `WorkListItem.id` = accession_number for insider), `skip_reason` (text — must be a member of `SignalSkipReason` union; engine does no validation but DB constraint may), `detail` (text), `recorded_at` (timestamptz). PK is `(run_id, ticker)` per the `onConflict` clause. **Q4 two-ledger semantics (verbatim from queue-config.ts:266-272 + insider-transactions.md:83):** the two ledgers answer DIFFERENT questions. (1) `signal_queue_skips` (ENGINE / item-scope / per-accession) — "did this work item ever succeed?" Records every permanent_skip with item-scope marker (synthetic ticker = accession). Telemetry-only; NOT read by the finalizer's mass-balance computation. (2) `loadAndCompute` return + `signal_compute_log.skips` (CONSUMER / name-scope / per-universe-ticker) — "did this universe ticker contribute to the 839 mass balance?" Every universe ticker MUST appear in `loadAndCompute`'s return as either `{kind:'value'}` or `{kind:'skip'}`. The two ledgers are NOT reconciled. An accession that hits `permanent_skip` writes to `signal_queue_skips` but its universe ticker (if any) is still expected to appear in `loadAndCompute`'s return — typically as `no_qualifying_transactions` if every accession for that ticker permanently skipped. **`SignalSkipReason` admissible values for insider work-list (from signal-types.ts:63-110):** at the item scope — `data_unavailable` (404/ambiguous primary/missing primary_doc), `fetch_error` (parse failure / generic catch), and the producer SHOULD propose a NEW reason `no_primary_doc` (already used in queue-work-list-mode_test.ts:350-367) for the ambiguous-primary path. See §(E) finding 2. |
| **§(D) — Producer-design crosswalk (each pipeline step mapped to its conforming contract; typed-permanent vs transient classification named at each call site)** | **Step D.1 — `seedWorkItems({asOf})` conforming to `WorkListSeedFn`:** Two modes via injected flag on the constructor: `mode:'daily'` (cron path) vs `mode:'backfill'` (manual-only path with `backfill: true`). **Date math — DAILY mode (worked example: asOf=2026-06-12T21:00:00Z, a Friday):** target = "yesterday's filing day" = `asOf − 1 trading day` = 2026-06-11 (Thursday). Fetch `dailyIndex.fetchDay(2026-06-11)` → `kind:'rows'`. Filter rows where (a) `form_type ∈ {'4','4/A'}` (already guaranteed by parser) AND (b) `padCik(filer_cik)` is in the universe inverse-map (built from `lookupTicker` per universe ticker → `padCik` reverse-index, with `kind:'unresolved'` tickers excluded and counted). For each surviving row emit `WorkListItem{ id: accession_number, payload: { filer_cik_raw, filer_cik_padded, ticker, date_filed } }`. If the day is a holiday (`kind:'unavailable'`), return `[]` — VALID empty-seed run per Q5; the next daily fire picks up. **Date math — BACKFILL mode (worked example: asOf=2026-06-12T21:00:00Z):** target = trailing 90 trading days from `asOf − 1` (so 2026-06-11 backward, weekends-only approximation per the daily-index fetcher's v1 rule). For each date D in the 90-day list, fetch `dailyIndex.fetchDay(D)`; unavailable days return `[]` and do NOT throw. Aggregate, dedup by `accession_number` (in case the operator runs backfill twice on overlapping windows — the work-list `id` uniqueness invariant per queue-work-list-mode_test.ts:275-292 forbids duplicate ids in one seed call), emit `WorkListItem[]`. **Pre-flight CIK map fetch is fetch-per-fire** (DEC-058 §(f1)) — called once at top of `seedWorkItems`, NOT inside the per-row loop. Throwing on CIK-map fetch propagates to `seed_failed` (Q5) — the run row inserts with `status:'failed'` and `failure_reason='seed_failed: <message>'`; NEVER half-seeded. **Step D.2 — `processItem({item, asOf})` conforming to `WorkListProcessItemFn`:** Pipeline per item: (D.2.a) `accessionIndex.fetchIndex({ cik: item.payload.filer_cik_padded, accession_number: item.id })`. Classification at this call site: `kind:'unavailable'` → `return { kind:'permanent_skip', reason:'data_unavailable', detail:'accession index 404' }` (permanent: SEC has no such accession, retry pointless). `kind:'rate_limited'` → THROW (transient: 429 — engine cursor preserved, slice retries naturally; the 5 rps bucket should already prevent this but the typed return is the safety net). `kind:'ambiguous'` → `return { kind:'permanent_skip', reason: 'no_primary_doc' (NEW — see §E finding 2), detail: \`eligible=\${eligible_count}; filenames=\${filenames.join(",")}\` }` (permanent: zero or many primary docs after exclusions, no heuristic tiebreak per INC-70). EdgarFetchError throw → propagates as transient (network / 403 UA / 5xx — the engine's 3-strikes deadlock guard handles repeated failure). `kind:'resolved'` → continue to D.2.b. (D.2.b) `form4Fetcher.fetch({ cik, accession_number, acceptance_datetime: from D.2.a, primary_document: from D.2.a })`. Classification: `kind:'unavailable'` → `permanent_skip` reason `data_unavailable` (404 on the XML — accession disappeared between index and XML fetch, treat as permanent). `kind:'rate_limited'` → THROW (transient). `kind:'unparseable'` → `permanent_skip` reason `fetch_error` detail prefixed `unparseable: \${reason}` (permanent: malformed XML or missing structural prerequisites won't fix on retry). `kind:'rows'; rows: []` → continue to D.2.c with empty rows (NOT a skip — this filing legitimately has zero non-derivative transactions; the upsert is a no-op and the cursor is deleted as `processed`). EdgarFetchError throw → propagates as transient. `kind:'rows'; rows: non-empty` → continue to D.2.c. (D.2.c) Per-row enforcement: every row MUST have non-empty `owner_cik` per the operator's 3.6b.iii′ ruling ("owner_cik absent → parse failure → permanent_skip" — this is the MIG-095 dual-write obligation). The parser's lenient `''` default (see §E finding 1) means the consumer MUST check `if (row.owner_cik === '') return { kind:'permanent_skip', reason:'fetch_error', detail: \`owner_cik absent on accession \${item.id} seq \${row.transaction_seq} — MIG-095 dual-write contract violated\` }` at this call site. (D.2.d) INC-74 batch-dedupe: dedup the row batch BEFORE upsert on the four-part §(h) key `(issuer_cik, owner_cik, transaction_date, transaction_seq)` — within ONE accession this should be a no-op (Form 4 emits each transaction once) but the dedupe enforces the unique-key invariant the table expects. (D.2.e) Upsert `insider_form4_rows` with `onConflict: 'issuer_cik,owner_cik,transaction_date,transaction_seq'` (or whatever the MIG-094/MIG-095 unique constraint is — re-verify at γ commit-1; see §E finding open). On upsert error throw (transient — Postgres temporal failures). On success `return { kind:'processed' }`. **Q1 barrier honored** because the upsert is performed BEFORE return. **Step D.3 — `loadAndCompute({asOf})` conforming to `WorkListLoadAndComputeFn`:** NEW thin function that runs steps 1-5 of the existing `createInsiderLoadAndCompute(ctx).run(as_of)` but STOPS before z-score + persist, returning `ReadonlyArray<{ ticker; gicsSector; result: TickerComputeResult }>` directly. See §E finding 3 for the contract-mismatch detail — the existing `.run()` does z-score + persist itself, which the engine finalizer also does, producing a DOUBLE-z-score + DOUBLE-persist if naively wired. **`accessionsPerSlice` arithmetic row (draft, conforming to FP-045 Phase 2 addendum §6 budget discipline):** `itemsPerSlice = 50`; `callsPerItem = 2` (accession `index.json` + Form-4 XML; the daily-index sweep + CIK map are seed-time, not per-item); `ratePerSec = 5 × 0.85 = 4.25` (SEC fair-access 10 rps half-share with 0.85 FP-045 safety multiplier; cite Phase-0 measurement at γ commit-1); rate-bound = `50 × 2 / 4.25 ≈ 23.5 s` per slice paced; add parser CPU + upsert wall ≈ 30-50 s total; safely under the 120 s STOP gate and 150 s HTTP wall. Daily fire: Phase-0 ~1,667 in-universe accessions/day → `⌈1667/50⌉ = 34 slices × ~30-50 s ≈ 17-28 min` queue-drained (matches the module-doc ~11.4 min figure if the per-slice wall trends to the low end; γ commit-1 pins the measured number). Backfill fire: ~150,030 accessions → `⌈150030/50⌉ = 3,001 slices × ~30-50 s ≈ 25-42 hours queue-drained`. **CRITICAL — backfill arithmetic mismatch from module doc:** module doc claims `~91k calls / ~5 hours`. Re-derive: 90 daily-index sweeps (seed-time, fast) + 150,030 accessions × 2 = 300,060 EDGAR calls (NOT 91k). At 5 rps that's `300060 / 5 = 60012 s ≈ 16.7 h` (NOT 5 h). The module doc's `~91k / ~5 h` figure is INCORRECT under the 2-calls-per-accession contract. See §E finding 4. Drift sentinel: `accessionsPerSlice_test.ts` (γ commit-1) asserts `itemsPerSlice * callsPerItem / ratePerSec < 60` as a structural sentinel; any future tightening of the rate cap or loosening of the slice size fails the test rather than silently breaching the wall. **`signal_id` and `job_id`:** `signalId = INSIDER_SIGNAL_ID = 'insider_transactions_90d'` (verified at insider-load-and-compute.ts:63); `jobId = 'longshort.insider.compute'` (verified at job-signal-mapping_test.ts (2d) + function-index.md:2960 — MIG-077 registered `enabled=false` schedule `'0 19 * * 1-5'`). |
| **§(E) — Contract mismatches surfaced (NOT patched here; resolved at γ commit-1 design)** | **Finding 1 — parser-side `owner_cik` lenient default vs. operator ruling.** `edgar-form4-parser.ts:173` returns `padCikString(firstTag(ownerIdBlock, 'rptOwnerCik'))` which silently emits `''` when the tag is absent (defensible at the parser level — keeps the parser pure-structural). But the operator 3.6b.iii′ ruling pins: "owner_cik absent → parse failure → permanent" (the MIG-095 dual-write obligation). **Two valid resolutions for γ commit-1:** (1a) tighten the parser to return `{kind:'unparseable', reason:'§(h) dual-key contract: rptOwnerCik missing on row N'}` — moves the contract to the structural layer; OR (1b) leave the parser lenient and enforce the contract at the consumer's D.2.c call site (per §D.2.c above) — moves the contract to the consumer where the MIG-095 binding lives. **Recommendation: (1b).** Rationale: the parser is shared (it produces the typed `EdgarForm4Row` shape that may have other future readers), the MIG-095 dual-write contract is consumer-specific (it exists because THIS consumer persists to the table that MIG-095 added the column to), and Constitution Rule 8 (additive correction, not structural supersession) favors enforcing new contracts at the new call site rather than rewriting shared layers. **Finding 2 — `signal_queue_skips.skip_reason = 'no_primary_doc'` is not in the `SignalSkipReason` union.** `signal-types.ts:63-110` enumerates `insufficient_history \| missing_sector \| fetch_error \| singleton_sector \| data_unavailable \| subscription_gated \| missing_shares_outstanding \| no_qualifying_transactions \| no_qualifying_flow \| no_recent_earnings \| ...`. The work-list test fixture already uses `'no_primary_doc'` (queue-work-list-mode_test.ts:350) — so the engine accepts it at runtime (no enum constraint in the slice-worker writer), but the type system rejects it on a strictly-typed `processItem` return. **Two valid resolutions for γ commit-1:** (2a) add `'no_primary_doc'` to `SignalSkipReason` (single-file edit to signal-types.ts) — clean, type-safe; OR (2b) reuse `'data_unavailable'` with `detail` carrying the disambiguation. **Recommendation: (2a)** — the work-list test fixture is the de-facto reference and already names this discriminator; the type system should agree with the runtime evidence. **Finding 3 — `createInsiderLoadAndCompute(ctx).run(as_of)` shape mismatch with `WorkListLoadAndComputeFn`.** The existing `.run()` returns `SignalOrchestratorResult` (z-scored, persisted to `signal_observations` via `captureSignalObservations`). The work-list engine's `loadAndCompute` contract returns `ReadonlyArray<{ ticker; gicsSector; result: TickerComputeResult }>` with PRE-z-score raw values and NO persist (queue-finalizer.ts:382-427 confirms the engine itself does z-score via `zScoreNormalizeWithinSector` at line 174 + persist via `captureSignalObservations` later). Wiring `.run()` naively as `loadAndCompute` would DOUBLE-z-score (mathematically wrong) and DOUBLE-persist (idempotency-relying-on-PK). **Resolution for γ commit-1:** factor the existing module to expose a NEW thin export — proposed `loadAndComputeRawSignals(ctx, asOf): Promise<ReadonlyArray<{ ticker; gicsSector; result: TickerComputeResult }>>` — that runs steps 1-5 (universe load, paginated 90d read, §(h) dedup, seam, per-ticker shares+price+`computeInsiderSignal` raw, typed-skip mapping) but RETURNS BEFORE step 6 (z-score) and step 7 (persist). The existing `.run()` continues to call the new function and add z-score + persist for any direct caller (note: post-3.6b.iii′ there are no direct callers in production; the function may become test-only). The work-list config's `loadAndCompute` field points at the new thin function. **Finding 4 — backfill arithmetic mismatch in `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/insider-transactions.md`.** Module doc lines 56 + 61 + 87 + 116 claim `~91k calls / ~5 hours` for the 90-day backfill. Re-deriving from the 2-calls-per-accession contract and Phase-0 ~1,667 in-universe accessions/day: `90 × 1667 × 2 = 300,060 EDGAR calls` at 5 rps = `~16.7 hours` queue-drained (NOT 91k / 5h). The `~91k` figure appears to count `90 (daily index) + ~1667 (in-universe accessions, single-day) + 90 × 1667 (per-accession index.json for backfill) ≈ 151k` — even that arithmetic doesn't reach 91k, and it omits the Form-4 XML fetch (the second of `callsPerItem=2`). **Resolution for γ commit-1:** correct the figure in the module-doc edit that accompanies the producer commit; cite the corrected arithmetic verbatim with `300,060 calls / ~16.7 h` and supersede the prior `~91k / ~5 h` claim per Rule 8 (historical citation preserved). The backfill-before-arm gate's WALL-CLOCK estimate moves from "~5 hours" to "~16-17 hours queue-drained" — operator must re-confirm the Phase 4 choreography timeline. **Finding 5 (open question for γ commit-1, NOT a mismatch).** The exact PK / unique constraint on `insider_form4_rows` should be re-verified from MIG-094 + MIG-095 source SQL before γ commit-1 wires the `onConflict` clause in D.2.e — module doc says `(issuer_cik, owner_cik, transaction_date, transaction_seq)` is the §(h) key but does not explicitly state whether that is a UNIQUE constraint vs. just the in-memory dedup key. If the table lacks the unique constraint, the upsert must either add it (new migration MIG-096) or use a different `onConflict` target (e.g., `accession_number, transaction_seq` if that's the actual unique index). **Recommendation:** re-read MIG-094 SQL at γ commit-1 start; if the constraint is absent, decide between MIG-096 (add unique) vs. consumer-side pre-upsert SELECT-then-INSERT (slower, no schema change). |

| Field | Value |
